.

A.3
Interpretation of Functions and Predicates

Array operators

standard read/update interpretation

Constructors

Fixed by the theory

Testers

Fixed by the theory

Selectors

s_{({c^{*}},i)}^{{\cal B}}(t)=\begin{cases}t_{i}&\text{if }t={c^{*}}(t_{1},\dots,t_{m})\text{ for some }t_{1},\ldots,t_{m}\\[4.0pt]
\Gamma_{\text{DT}}\!\bigl(s_{(c,i)}^{{\cal A}}(s)\bigr)&\text{otherwise, if there is }s\in\sigma^{{\cal A}}\text{ s.t.}\Gamma_{\text{DT}}(s)=t\\[4.0pt]
\mathbf{d}_{{\sigma_{i}^{*}}}&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

UF symbols

f_{A_{j}}^{{\cal B}}\bigl(c_{j}(t_{1},\dots,t_{k})\bigr)=\Gamma_{\text{Arr}}(t_{1})

g_{A_{j}}^{{\cal B}}(t)=\begin{cases}\Gamma_{\text{DT}}(a)&\text{if }a\in A_{j}^{{\cal A}}\text{ and }\Gamma_{\text{Arr}}(a)=t,\\[4.0pt]
\mathbf{d}_{{A_{j}^{*}}}&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Table 17:
Interpretation of function and predicate symbols,
where
\mathbf{d}_{{\sigma_{i}^{*}}}
is an arbitrary (but fixed) element of

{\sigma_{i}^{*}}^{{\cal B}}
.

The interpretation of function and predicate symbols
in
{\cal B}
is defined in
Table

17
and
described as follows.

Array operations.

The symbols
\textbf{select}_{A_{j}^{\prime}}
and

\textbf{store}_{A_{j}^{\prime}}
are interpreted in the standard way,
as read and update operations over functions.

Datatype symbols.

The interpretation of
constructors and testers is fixed, as we require

{\cal B}^{{{\Sigma_{n+1}^{{{\phi}}}}^{*}}}
to be a datatypes structure.
As for selectors, their interpretation is fixed when applied
to terms that are constructed by their corresponding constructors.
When applied to different constructors, we maintain the connection
to
{\cal A}
as induced by
\Gamma_{\text{DT}}
, or set them
arbitrarily if no such connection is induced.
Injectivity of
\Gamma_{\text{DT}}
guarantees that this definition is unambiguous.

Uninterpreted functions.

Let
1\leq j\leq n
.
We define

f_{A_{j}}^{{\cal B}}:{A_{j}^{*}}^{{\cal B}}\rightarrow A_{j}^{\prime{\cal B}}

and

g_{A_{j}}^{{\cal B}}:A_{j}^{\prime{\cal B}}\rightarrow{A_{j}^{*}}^{{\cal B}}

as follows.
For
f_{A_{j}}
,
each element of
{A_{j}^{*}}^{{\cal B}}

has the form
c_{j}(t_{1},\ldots,t_{k})
,
where
t_{1}\in{{{A_{j}}^{\circ}}}^{{\cal B}}=A_{j}^{{\cal A}}
.
We then take
t_{1}
and pass it through
\Gamma_{\text{Arr}}
.
For
g_{A_{j}}
,
given
t\in A_{j}^{\prime}
we consider two cases.
In the first, there exists
a\in A_{j}^{{\cal A}}

with
\Gamma_{\text{Arr}}(a)=t
, and then we return

\Gamma_{\text{DT}}(a)
.
If there is no such
a
, we return an arbitrary but fixed element

\mathbf{d}_{{A_{j}^{*}}}
of

{A_{j}^{*}}^{{\cal B}}
.
Injectivity of
\Gamma_{\text{Arr}}
guarantees that the definition of
g_{A_{j}}^{{\cal B}}
is unambiguous.

A.4
{\cal B}
is a
T_{\text{new}}^{{{\phi}}}
-interpretation

Now that the construction of
{\cal B}
is done, we prove that
it is indeed a
T_{\text{new}}^{{{\phi}}}
-interpretation.

Lemma A.56
.

{\cal B}
is a
T_{\text{new}}^{{{\phi}}}
-interpretation.

Proof A.57
.

Recall that

T_{\text{new}}^{{{\phi}}}\;=\;\Bigl(\biguplus_{j=1}^{n}{T_{j}^{*}}\Bigr)\;\uplus\;T_{n+1}^{{\phi}}\;\uplus\;T_{n+2}\,.

For each
j\in[1,n]
, the reduct
{\cal B}^{{\Sigma_{j}^{*}}}
interprets
\textbf{select}_{A_{j}^{\prime}}
and
\textbf{store}_{A_{j}^{\prime}}
in the standard way for an arrays structure.
On
\Sigma_{n+1}^{{\phi}}
,
{\cal B}
uses the usual tree‐based interpretation of
constructors, selectors and testers (see Definition
6
). By
construction, each selector and tester in
\Sigma_{n+1}^{{\phi}}
obeys the requirements of datatypes structures.
Finally, on
\Sigma_{n+2}
we only have the symbols
f_{A_{j}}
and
g_{A_{j}}
,
and there is no restriction on their interpretation.

A.5
Satisfaction of the Translated Literals

Lemma A.58
.

Let
l
be a
\Sigma
-literal and let
\bf{tr}(l)
be its translation.
If
{\cal A}\models l
then
{\cal B}\models\bf{tr}(l)
.

Proof A.59
.

We go over all possible shapes of
l
.

1.

x=y
.

Since
x^{{\cal A}}=y^{{\cal A}}
,

\Gamma_{\text{DT}}(x^{{\cal A}})=\Gamma_{\text{DT}}(y^{{\cal A}})
,
hence
{x^{*}}^{{\cal B}}={y^{*}}^{{\cal B}}
.

2.

x\neq y
.

x^{{\cal A}}\neq y^{{\cal A}}
and injectivity of
\Gamma_{\text{DT}}
gives
{x^{*}}^{{\cal B}}=\Gamma_{\text{DT}}(x^{\cal A})\neq\Gamma_{\text{DT}}(y^{\cal A})={y^{*}}^{{\cal B}}
.

3.

x=\textbf{select}_{A}(a,i)
.

In
{\cal A}
we have

x^{{\cal A}}=\textbf{select}_{A}^{{\cal A}}(a^{{\cal A}},i^{{\cal A}})
.
Therefore

\textbf{select}_{A^{\prime}}^{{\cal B}}(a^{\prime{\cal B}},{i^{*}}^{{\cal B}})=a^{\prime{\cal B}}({i^{*}}^{{\cal B}})=\Gamma_{\text{DT}}\Bigl(\textbf{select}_{A}^{\cal A}(a^{\cal A},i^{\cal A})\Bigr)=\Gamma_{\text{DT}}(x^{\cal A})={x^{*}}^{{\cal B}}.

The first equality is by the definition of
\textbf{select}_{A^{\prime}}^{{\cal B}}
, the second one is by the definition of
a^{\prime{\cal B}}
, the third one is by the substitution of
\textbf{select}_{A}^{\cal A}(a^{\cal A},i^{\cal A})
with
x^{\cal A}
, and the last one is by the definition of
{x^{*}}^{{\cal B}}
.

4.

b=\textbf{store}_{A_{l}}(a,i,v)
.

As
b^{\cal A}=\textbf{store}_{A_{l}}^{\cal A}(a^{\cal A},i^{\cal A},v^{\cal A})
, it follows that:

(a)

\textbf{select}_{A_{l}}^{\cal A}(b^{\cal A},i^{\cal A})=v^{\cal A}
, and

(b)

for all
j\in I_{l}^{\cal A}\setminus\{i^{\cal A}\}
,
\textbf{select}_{A_{l}}^{\cal A}(b^{\cal A},j)=\textbf{select}_{A_{l}}^{\cal A}(a^{\cal A},j)
.

Then, by the new interpretation we have:

(a)

b^{\prime{\cal B}}({i^{*}}^{{\cal B}})=\Gamma_{\text{DT}}\bigl(\textbf{select}_{A_{l}}^{\cal A}(b^{\cal A},i^{\cal A})\bigr)=\Gamma_{\text{DT}}(v^{\cal A})={v^{*}}^{{\cal B}}=\textbf{store}_{A_{l}^{\prime}}^{{\cal B}}(a^{\prime{\cal B}},{i^{*}}^{{\cal B}},{v^{*}}^{{\cal B}})({i^{*}}^{\cal B})
,

(b)

For every
j\in{I_{l}^{*}}^{\cal B}\setminus\{i^{\cal B}\}
,

b^{\prime{\cal B}}(j)=\Gamma_{\text{DT}}\bigl(\textbf{select}_{A_{l}}^{\cal A}(b^{\cal A},j)\bigr)=\Gamma_{\text{DT}}\bigl(\textbf{select}_{A_{l}}^{\cal A}(a^{\cal A},j)\bigr)=\textbf{select}_{A_{l}^{\prime}}^{{\cal B}}(a^{\prime{\cal B}},j)=a^{\prime{\cal B}}(j)
,

Hence,
\forall j\in{I_{l}^{*}}^{\cal B}.b^{\prime{\cal B}}(j)=\textbf{store}_{A_{l}^{\prime}}^{{\cal B}}(a^{\prime{\cal B}},{i^{*}}^{{\cal B}},{v^{*}}^{{\cal B}})(j)
,
and thus,
{b^{*}}^{{\cal B}}=\textbf{store}_{A_{l}^{\prime}}^{{\cal B}}(a^{\prime{\cal B}},{i^{*}}^{{\cal B}},{v^{*}}^{{\cal B}})
.

5.

x=s_{(c,i)}(y)
.

As
x^{\cal A}=s_{(c,i)}^{\cal A}(y^{\cal A})
, we distinguish two cases:

(a)

Case 1:
If
y^{\cal A}
is an application of
c
, then we can denote

y^{\cal A}=c(t_{1},\dots,t_{m})
.
Since
{\cal A}^{\Sigma_{n+1}}
is a datatypes
\Sigma_{n+1}
-structure, and by
Definition

6
:

x^{\cal A}=t_{i}
.
Consequently, according to
Remark

A.46

{y^{*}}^{{\cal B}}=\Gamma_{\text{DT}}(y^{\cal A})={c^{*}}\bigl(\Gamma_{\text{DT}}(t_{1}),\dots,\Gamma_{\text{DT}}(t_{m})\bigr),

and applying the new selector yields

s_{({c^{*}},i)}^{{\cal B}}({y^{*}}^{{\cal B}})=\Gamma_{\text{DT}}(t_{i})=\Gamma_{\text{DT}}(x^{\cal A})={x^{*}}^{{\cal B}}.

(b)

Case 2:
If
y^{\cal A}
is not an application of
c
, then

s_{({c^{*}},i)}^{{\cal B}}({y^{*}}^{{\cal B}})=\Gamma_{\text{DT}}\Bigl(s_{(c,i)}^{\cal A}\bigl(y^{\cal A}\bigr)\Bigr)=\Gamma_{\text{DT}}\Bigl(x^{\cal A}\Bigr)={x^{*}}^{{\cal B}}

In both cases, the equality

{x^{*}}^{{\cal B}}=s_{({c^{*}},i)}^{{\cal B}}({y^{*}}^{{\cal B}})

holds.

6.

x=c(t_{1},\dots,t_{m})
.

Since
x^{\cal A}=c(t_{1}^{\cal A},\ldots,t_{m}^{\cal A})
,
Remark

A.46
suggests that

{x^{*}}^{{\cal B}}=\Gamma_{\text{DT}}(x^{\cal A})=\Gamma_{\text{DT}}(c(t_{1}^{\cal A},\ldots,t_{m}^{\cal A}))={c^{*}}\bigl(\Gamma_{\text{DT}}(t_{1}^{\cal A}),\ldots,\Gamma_{\text{DT}}(t_{m}^{\cal A})\bigr)={c^{*}}\bigl({t_{1}^{*}}^{{\cal B}},\ldots,{t_{m}^{*}}^{{\cal B}}\bigr).

7.

\text{is}_{c}(x)
or
\neg\text{is}_{c}(x)
.

Whether
x^{{\cal A}}
is (or is not) a
c
-term is preserved by

\Gamma_{\text{DT}}
, so the corresponding tester

\text{is}_{{c^{*}}}
(or its negation) is satisfied in
{\cal B}
.

Hence every literal of
\bf{trFormula}({{\phi}})
is true in
{\cal B}
, so

{\cal B}\models\bf{trFormula}({{\phi}})
.

A.6
Satisfaction of the Lemmas

A.6.1
Satisfaction of
\mathbf{L_{1}}({{{\phi}}})
and
\mathbf{L_{2}}({{{\phi}}})

Fix an array variable
a\in{\it vars}_{A_{j}}({{{\phi}}})
(for some
j\in[1,n]
).
Let

\iota_{1},\dots,\iota_{n_{(A_{j},{{\phi}})}}\quad\text{be the indices in }{\mathbb{I}}_{(A_{j},{{\phi}})}\text{ ordered by }o_{(A_{j},{{\phi}})}.

For each
k\in[1,n_{(A_{j},{{\phi}})}]
the block

\mathbf{L_{0}^{{{\phi}}}}({a,\iota_{k}})=\{\,u_{(a,k)}=\textbf{select}_{A_{j}^{\prime}}(a^{\prime},{\iota_{k}^{*}}),\;u_{(a,k)}=s_{(c_{j},\,k+1)}({a^{*}})\,\}

is satisfied in
{\cal B}
:

u_{(a,k)}^{\cal B}=\Gamma_{\text{DT}}\Bigl(\textbf{select}_{A_{j}}^{\cal A}\bigl(a^{\cal A},\iota_{k}^{\cal A}\bigr)\Bigr)=a^{\prime{\cal B}}({\iota_{k}^{*}}^{\cal B})=\textbf{select}_{A_{j}^{\prime}}^{{\cal B}}\bigl(a^{\prime{\cal B}},{\iota_{k}^{*}}^{\cal B}\bigr),

Also, the interpretation of
{a^{*}}
in
{\cal B}
is defined as follows:

{a^{*}}^{{\cal B}}=c_{j}\Bigl(a^{\cal A},\Gamma_{\text{DT}}\Bigl(\textbf{select}_{A_{j}}^{\cal A}\bigl(a^{\cal A},\iota_{1}^{\cal A}\bigr)\Bigr),\ldots,\Gamma_{\text{DT}}\Bigl(\textbf{select}_{A_{j}}^{\cal A}\bigl(a^{\cal A},\iota_{n_{(A_{j},{{\phi}})}}^{\cal A}\bigr)\Bigr)\Bigr).

Hence, it follows that

u_{(a,k)}^{\cal B}=\Gamma_{\text{DT}}\Bigl(\textbf{select}_{A_{j}}^{\cal A}\bigl(a^{\cal A},\iota_{k}^{\cal A}\bigr)\Bigr)=s_{(c_{j},k+1)}^{{\cal B}}({a^{*}}^{{\cal B}})

and thus
{\cal B}\models\mathbf{L_{0}^{{{\phi}}}}({a,\iota_{k}})
.

Note that

\mathbf{L_{1}}({{{\phi}}})\cup\mathbf{L_{2}}({{{\phi}}})\subseteq\bigcup_{a\in{\it vars}_{A_{j}}({{{\phi}}})}\bigcup_{i\in{\mathbb{I}}_{(A_{j},{{\phi}})}}\mathbf{L_{0}^{{{\phi}}}}({a,i}).

Thus, we have shown that for every literal
l\in\mathbf{L_{1}}({{{\phi}}})\cup\mathbf{L_{2}}({{{\phi}}})
, we have
{\cal B}\models l
.

A.6.2
Satisfaction of
\mathbf{L_{3}}({{{\phi}}})

Let
a\in{\it vars}_{A_{j}}({{{\phi}}})
for some
j\in[1,n]
.
Note that:

f_{A_{j}}^{{\cal B}}({a^{*}}^{{\cal B}})=f_{A_{j}}^{{\cal B}}(c_{j}(a^{\cal A},\ldots))=\Gamma_{\text{Arr}}(a^{\cal A})=a^{\prime{\cal B}}

And:

g_{A_{j}}^{{\cal B}}(a^{\prime{\cal B}})=g_{A_{j}}^{{\cal B}}(\Gamma_{\text{Arr}}(a^{\cal A}))=\Gamma_{\text{DT}}(a^{\cal A})={a^{*}}^{{\cal B}}

Thus,
\forall l\in\mathbf{L_{3}}({{{\phi}}}).{\cal B}\models l
.

Appendix B
Completeness

In what follows,

\Sigma
continues to be the NDT signature of
\Sigma_{1},\ldots,\Sigma_{n+1}
,
where
\Sigma_{1},\dots,\Sigma_{n}
are arrays signatures and
\Sigma_{n+1}
is a datatypes signature.
In contrast to
Theorem

35
, the
completeness result assumes that
T_{n+1}

is stably infinite w.r.t.

\big(\bigcup_{i=1}^{n}{\cal S}_{\Sigma_{i}}\big)\cap{\cal S}_{\Sigma_{n+1}}
,
a.k.a.
w.r.t. its
shared sorts
.

Theorem B.60
.

Let
\Sigma
be the NDT signature of

\Sigma_{1},\dots,\Sigma_{n+1}
such that
T_{n+1}
is stably infinite w.r.t.
\big(\bigcup_{i=1}^{n}{\cal S}_{\Sigma_{i}}\big)\cap{\cal S}_{\Sigma_{n+1}}
and let

{{\phi}}
be a flat
\Sigma
-cube such that
\bf{trFormula}({{\phi}})
is

T_{\text{new}}^{{{\phi}}}
-satisfiable.
Then
{{\phi}}
is
{{\cal T}}_{{\Sigma}}^{{\mathit{N}DT}}
-satisfiable.

The main idea of this section is to generalize the construction of the interpretation presented in
Table

13
.

Domains

{I^{*}}^{\hat{{\cal B}}}=\mathbb{N}

{{{A}^{\circ}}}^{\hat{{\cal B}}}=\{\gamma\}

{E^{*}}^{\hat{{\cal B}}}=T_{{E^{*}}}\bigl({\Sigma_{\mathbf{ndt}}^{{{\psi}}}}_{\mid{{\cal C}}{{\cal O}}_{\Sigma_{\mathbf{ndt}}^{{{\psi}}}}},\,B\bigr)

{A^{*}}^{\hat{{\cal B}}}=T_{{A^{*}}}\bigl({\Sigma_{\mathbf{ndt}}^{{{\psi}}}}_{\mid{{\cal C}}{{\cal O}}_{\Sigma_{\mathbf{ndt}}^{{{\psi}}}}},\,B\bigr)

A^{\prime\hat{{\cal B}}}={I^{*}}^{\hat{{\cal B}}}\;\to\;{E^{*}}^{\hat{{\cal B}}}

Functions

\textbf{select},\textbf{store}
: standard read/update on
A^{\prime\hat{{\cal B}}}
.

{{\mathbf{c}}^{*}}^{\hat{{\cal B}}}(n,y)={{\mathbf{c}}^{*}}(n,y)

{{\mathbf{emp}}^{*}}^{\hat{{\cal B}}}={{\mathbf{emp}}^{*}}

{{\mathbf{id}}^{*}}^{\hat{{\cal B}}}({{\mathbf{c}}^{*}}(n,y))=n
,
{{\mathbf{chld}}^{*}}^{\hat{{\cal B}}}({{\mathbf{c}}^{*}}(m,y))=y

{{\mathbf{id}}^{*}}^{\hat{{\cal B}}}({{\mathbf{emp}}^{*}})=0
,
{{\mathbf{chld}}^{*}}^{\hat{{\cal B}}}({{\mathbf{emp}}^{*}})=\textit{const}({{{\mathbf{emp}}^{*}}})

f_{A}^{\hat{{\cal B}}}
: constant function returning
a^{\prime\hat{{\cal B}}}
.

g_{A}^{\hat{{\cal B}}}
: constant function returning
{a^{*}}^{\hat{{\cal B}}}
.

Predicates

\text{is}_{{{\mathbf{c}}^{*}}}^{\hat{{\cal B}}}({{\mathbf{c}}^{*}}(n,y))=\text{true}
,

\text{is}_{{{\mathbf{c}}^{*}}}^{\hat{{\cal B}}}({{\mathbf{emp}}^{*}})=\text{false}
,

\text{is}_{{{\mathbf{emp}}^{*}}}^{\hat{{\cal B}}}({{\mathbf{emp}}^{*}})=\text{true}
,

\text{is}_{{{\mathbf{emp}}^{*}}}^{\hat{{\cal B}}}({{\mathbf{c}}^{*}}(n,y))=\text{false}
.

Variables

{x^{*}}^{\hat{{\cal B}}}\;=\;u_{(a,1)}^{\hat{{\cal B}}}={{\mathbf{c}}^{*}}\bigl(3,\;c_{1}(\gamma,\;{{\mathbf{emp}}^{*}})\bigr)

a^{\prime\hat{{\cal B}}}=\textit{const}({{x^{*}}^{\hat{{\cal B}}}})=\textit{const}({{{\mathbf{c}}^{*}}(3,c_{1}(\gamma,{{\mathbf{emp}}^{*}}))})

{j^{*}}^{\hat{{\cal B}}}={k^{*}}^{\hat{{\cal B}}}=1

{a^{*}}^{\hat{{\cal B}}}=c_{1}\bigl(\gamma,\;{{\mathbf{c}}^{*}}(3,c_{1}(\gamma,{{\mathbf{emp}}^{*}}))\bigr)

{y^{*}}^{\hat{{\cal B}}}={{\mathbf{c}}^{*}}\bigl(1,\;c_{1}(\gamma,{{\mathbf{c}}^{*}}(3,c_{1}(\gamma,{{\mathbf{emp}}^{*}})))\bigr)

Table 18:
A
\Sigma_{\mathbf{ndt}}^{{{\psi}}}
–interpretation,
\hat{{\cal B}}
, that satisfies
\bf{trFormula}({{\psi}})
, from
Table

12
.
Here
B
is the
{\bf Elem}_{\Sigma_{\mathbf{ndt}}^{{{\psi}}}}
–sorted set with
B_{\rho}=\rho^{\hat{{\cal B}}}
for each
\rho\in{\bf Elem}_{\Sigma_{\mathbf{ndt}}^{{{\psi}}}}
.

Example B.61
.

Consider the formula
{{\psi}}
from

Table

12
, and its translation
\bf{trFormula}({{\psi}})
, also from

Table

12
.
We already know from
Example

36
that
{{\psi}}
is

{{\cal T}}_{{\Sigma_{\mathbf{ndt}}}}^{{\mathit{N}DT}}
-satisfiable,
and from
Theorem

35
we get that

\bf{trFormula}({{\psi}})
is
T_{\text{new}}^{{{\psi}}}
-satisfiable.
We reuse these formulas in order to provide examples
for the steps of our completeness proofs.
Since our starting point should be a

T_{\text{new}}^{{{\psi}}}
-interpretation that satisfies
\bf{trFormula}({{\psi}})
,
which may not be the one constructed in
Appendix

A
,
we define in
Table

18
a new
T_{\text{new}}^{{{\psi}}}
-interpretation,
not obtained from the process of
Appendix

A
,
that satisfies
\bf{trFormula}({{\psi}})
.
We shall later see that
{{\psi}}
is
{{\cal T}}_{{\Sigma_{\mathbf{ndt}}}}^{{\mathit{N}DT}}
-satisfiable, by constructing a satisfying
{{\cal T}}_{{\Sigma_{\mathbf{ndt}}}}^{{\mathit{N}DT}}
-interpretation
based on the one from
Table

18
.
In turn, this satisfying interpretation will be different
from the one shown in
Table

13
.

Domains

{{{A}^{\circ}}}^{{\cal B}}=\{\gamma,\delta\}

A^{\prime{\cal B}}

{{\mathbf{c}}^{*}}^{{\cal B}}(y)={{\mathbf{c}}^{*}}(y)

,

\text{is}_{{{\mathbf{emp}}^{*}}}^{{\cal B}}({{\mathbf{emp}}^{*}})=\text{true}
,

\text{is}_{{{\mathbf{emp}}^{*}}}^{{\cal B}}({{\mathbf{emp}}^{*}})=\text{true}
.

Variables

{x^{*}}^{{\cal B}}\;=\;{{\mathbf{c}}^{*}}(c_{1}(\gamma))

{y^{*}}^{{\cal B}}\;=\;{{\mathbf{c}}^{*}}(c_{1}(\delta))

Table 19:
A
\Sigma_{\mathbf{ndt}}^{{{\psi}}}
–interpretation
{\cal B}
satisfying

{x^{*}}\neq{y^{*}}
.
Here
B
is the
{\bf Elem}_{\Sigma_{\mathbf{ndt}}^{{{\psi}}}}
–sorted set with
B_{\rho}=\rho^{{\cal B}}
for each
\rho\in{\bf Elem}_{\Sigma_{\mathbf{ndt}}^{{{\psi}}}}
.

Roadmap.

Assume
{\cal B}\models\bf{trFormula}({{\phi}})
for some arbitrary

T_{\text{new}}^{{{\phi}}}
-interpretation
{\cal B}
.
The goal is, of course, to construct
a

{{\cal T}}_{{\Sigma}}^{{\mathit{N}DT}}
-interpretation
{\cal A}
that satisfies
{{\phi}}
.
This is carried out in steps:

(i)

Repairing
{\cal B}
.

Starting from the given
T_{\text{new}}^{{{\phi}}}
-interpretation
{\cal B}
,

Section

B.1
builds a well-behaved
T_{\text{new}}^{{{\phi}}}
-interpretation that still satisfies
\bf{trFormula}({{\phi}})
.

(ii)

Defining the domains.

Section

B.2
defines the interpretation of every
sort in the sought
{{\cal T}}_{{\Sigma}}^{{\mathit{N}DT}}
-interpretation
{\cal A}
.
In
Section

B.3
we prove several useful properties of these domains.

(iii)

Guaranteeing infinite
|E_{i}^{\cal A}|
.

Section

B.4
shows that
\forall i\in[1,n].|E_{i}^{{\cal A}}|=\infty
. This is needed in order to define the interpretation of the variables.

(iv)

Defining the interpretation of the symbols.

In
Section

B.5
–
Table

23
, we define the interpretation of the functions, predicates, and variables in
{\cal A}
.

(v)

Proving that
{\cal A}
is a

{{\cal T}}_{{\Sigma}}^{{\mathit{N}DT}}
-interpretation.

In
Section

B.7
, we show that the
interpretation
{\cal A}
is a
{{\cal T}}_{{\Sigma}}^{{\mathit{N}DT}}
-interpretation.

(vi)

Checking literal satisfaction.

Finally, in
Section

B.8
, we show that the interpretation
{\cal A}
satisfies every literal in
{{\phi}}
.

B.1
Creating a well-behaved interpretation

Given a
T_{\text{new}}^{{{\phi}}}
-interpretation
{\cal B}
,
that satisfies
\bf{trFormula}({{\phi}})
,
our overall goal is to construct a

{{\cal T}}_{{\Sigma}}^{{\mathit{N}DT}}
-interpretation
{\cal A}
that satisfies
{{\phi}}
.
Our first step is to transform
{\cal B}
to another interpretation that
is more comfortable to work with.
Namely, we construct a
T_{\text{new}}^{{{\phi}}}
-interpretation that satisfies

\bf{trFormula}({{\phi}})
and satisfies the following two requirements:

(i)
the domains of all the sorts that are not in
{\bf Struct}_{\Sigma_{n+1}^{{\phi}}}\cup{\bf{Array}}_{\Sigma^{{{\phi}}}}
(
{\bf{Array}}_{newSig{\Sigma}}=\{A_{i}^{\prime}\mid 1\leq i\leq n\}
, see
1
) are infinite; and

(ii)
the domains of array sorts consist of functions from
the domains of their index sorts to the domains of their
element sorts
(while this is typically the “intended” semantics of arrays,
other interpretations can be built that satisfy the axioms of the theory).

We start with the following lemma, that establishes
a simple property of datatype structures.

Lemma B.62
.

Let
\Sigma_{\text{DT}}
be a datatype signature, and
let
\bar{B}
and
\tilde{B}
be
{\bf Elem}_{\Sigma_{\text{DT}}}
-sorted sets with

\bar{B}_{\tau}\subseteq\tilde{B}_{\tau}
for every
\tau\in{\bf Elem}_{\Sigma_{\text{DT}}}
.
Then for all
\sigma\in{\cal S}_{\Sigma_{\text{DT}}}

T_{\sigma}\bigl({\Sigma_{\text{DT}}}_{\mid{{\cal C}}{{\cal O}}_{\Sigma_{\text{DT}}}},\bar{B}\bigr)\;\subseteq\;T_{\sigma}\bigl({\Sigma_{\text{DT}}}_{\mid{{\cal C}}{{\cal O}}_{\Sigma_{\text{DT}}}},\tilde{B}\bigr).

Proof B.63
.

First, we denote
\widetilde{\Sigma_{\text{DT}}}:={\Sigma_{\text{DT}}}_{\mid{{\cal C}}{{\cal O}}_{\Sigma_{\text{DT}}}}
for brevity.
If
\sigma\in{\bf Elem}
, then by definition, for every
k
,

T_{\sigma,k}(\widetilde{\Sigma_{\text{DT}}},\bar{B})=\bar{B}_{\sigma}\subseteq\tilde{B}_{\sigma}=T_{\sigma,k}(\widetilde{\Sigma_{\text{DT}}},\tilde{B}).

Now let
\sigma\in{\bf Struct}
. We argue by induction on
k
that
T_{\sigma,k}\bigl(\widetilde{\Sigma_{\text{DT}}},\bar{B}\bigr)\;\subseteq\;T_{\sigma,k}\bigl(\widetilde{\Sigma_{\text{DT}}},\tilde{B}\bigr).

Base (
k=0
).

T_{\sigma,0}(\widetilde{\Sigma_{\text{DT}}},\bar{B})=\emptyset\subseteq T_{\sigma,0}(\widetilde{\Sigma_{\text{DT}}},\tilde{B}).

Step.
Suppose

T_{\tau,k}(\widetilde{\Sigma_{\text{DT}}},\bar{B})\subseteq T_{\tau,k}(\widetilde{\Sigma_{\text{DT}}},\tilde{B})

for every
\tau
. Then

T_{\sigma,k+1}(\widetilde{\Sigma_{\text{DT}}},\bar{B})=T_{\sigma,k}(\widetilde{\Sigma_{\text{DT}}},\bar{B})\;\cup\;\bigl\{c(t_{1},\dots,t_{n})\mid c:\sigma_{1}\times\cdots\times\sigma_{n}\to\sigma,\;t_{j}\in T_{\sigma_{j},k}(\widetilde{\Sigma_{\text{DT}}},\bar{B})\bigr\}.

By the IH each
t_{j}\in T_{\sigma_{j},k}(\widetilde{\Sigma_{\text{DT}}},\tilde{B})
,
so each
c(t_{1},\dots,t_{n})
lies in

T_{\sigma,k+1}(\widetilde{\Sigma_{\text{DT}}},\tilde{B})
.
Hence

T_{\sigma}(\widetilde{\Sigma_{\text{DT}}},\bar{B})=\bigcup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}T_{\sigma,k}(\widetilde{\Sigma_{\text{DT}}},\bar{B})\subseteq\bigcup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}T_{\sigma,k}(\widetilde{\Sigma_{\text{DT}}},\tilde{B})=T_{\sigma}(\widetilde{\Sigma_{\text{DT}}},\tilde{B})

Sort (in
{\cal S}_{\Sigma^{{{\phi}}}}
)

Original sort (in
{\cal S}_{\Sigma}
)

Domain chosen in
{\cal C}

{{A_{i}}^{\circ}}

A_{i}\in{\bf{Array}}_{\Sigma}

{{A_{i}}^{\circ}}^{{\cal C}}=\begin{cases}{{A_{i}}^{\circ}}^{{\cal B}}\cup\{e_{k}^{{A_{i}}^{\circ}}\mid k\in\mathbb{N}\}&\text{if }|{{A_{i}}^{\circ}}^{{\cal B}}|<\infty\\[6.0pt]
{{A_{i}}^{\circ}}^{{\cal B}}&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

{\tau^{*}}

\tau\in{\cal S}_{\Sigma}\setminus({\bf{Array}}_{\Sigma}\cup{\bf Struct}_{\Sigma_{n+1}})

{\tau^{*}}^{\cal C}=\begin{cases}{\tau^{*}}^{{\cal B}}\cup\{e_{k}^{{\tau^{*}}}\mid k\in\mathbb{N}\}&\text{if }|{\tau^{*}}^{{\cal B}}|<\infty\\[6.0pt]
{\tau^{*}}^{{\cal B}}&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

\tau\in{\bf Struct}_{\Sigma_{n+1}}\cup{\bf{Array}}_{\Sigma}

\displaystyle{\tau^{*}}^{{\cal C}}=T_{{\tau^{*}}}\bigl({\Sigma_{n+1}^{{{\phi}}}}_{\mid{{\cal C}}{{\cal O}}_{\Sigma_{n+1}^{{{\phi}}}}},\,B\bigr)

A_{i}^{\prime}

A_{i}\in{\bf{Array}}_{\Sigma}

\displaystyle A_{i}^{\prime{\cal C}}={I_{i}^{*}}^{{\cal C}}\!\to\!{E_{i}^{*}}^{{\cal C}}

Table 20:
Interpretations of the sorts in
{\cal C}
.
The set
\{e_{k}^{\sigma}\mid k\in\mathbb{N}\}

consists of countably many fresh
elements.

B
is the
{\bf Elem}_{\Sigma^{{{\phi}}}}
-sorted set with

B_{\rho}=\rho^{{\cal C}}
for every

\rho\in{\bf Elem}_{\Sigma^{{{\phi}}}}
.

Lemma B.64
.

Let
{\cal B}
be a
T_{\text{new}}^{{{\phi}}}
-interpretation that satisfies a flat
\Sigma^{{{\phi}}}
-cube
\theta
.
Then, there exists
{\cal C}
, a
T_{\text{new}}^{{{\phi}}}
-interpretation that satisfies
\theta
such that:

1.

\forall\sigma\in{\cal S}_{\Sigma^{{{\phi}}}}\setminus({\bf Struct}_{\Sigma_{n+1}^{{\phi}}}\cup{\bf{Array}}_{\Sigma^{{{\phi}}}}).|\sigma^{{\cal C}}|=\infty
.

2.

\forall i\in[1,n].A_{i}^{\prime{\cal C}}={I_{i}^{*}}^{{\cal C}}\to{E_{i}^{*}}^{{\cal C}}
.

3.

The
\textbf{select}_{A_{i}^{\prime}}
and
\textbf{store}_{A_{i}^{\prime}}
functions are interpreted in the usual way for every
i\in[1,n]
.

Proof B.65
.

W.l.o.g., we can assume that the domains in
{\cal B}
of all the sorts in

{\cal S}_{\Sigma^{{{\phi}}}}\setminus({\bf Struct}_{\Sigma_{n+1}^{{\phi}}}\cup{\bf{Array}}_{\Sigma^{{{\phi}}}})
are completely fresh and flat
(i.e., they in particular do not contain any constructor symbols in
them, or more specifically, we may assume that they are “copies” of subsets of the natural numbers).
In order to show the existence of such
{\cal C}
, we go through the following
steps:

1.

We define the domains of the sorts in
{\cal C}
.

2.

We define the interpretations of the symbols in
{\cal C}
.

3.

We show that
{\cal C}
is a
T_{\text{new}}^{{{\phi}}}
-interpretation.

4.

We show that every literal in
{{\phi}}
is satisfied by
{\cal C}
.

1. Defining the domains.

The domains of the sorts in
\Sigma^{{{\phi}}}
are defined in
Table

20
.
Let there be
\sigma\in{\cal S}_{\Sigma^{{{\phi}}}}
. We have three cases to consider:

1.

If
\sigma={{A_{i}}^{\circ}}
for some
i\in[1,n]

then
\sigma
is interpreted in
{\cal C}
exactly in the same way it is interpreted in
{\cal B}
if its domain is already infinite.
Otherwise, we add infinitely many fresh elements to it, denoted as
\{e_{k}^{{{A_{i}}^{\circ}}}\mid k\in\mathbb{N}\}
.

2.

If
\sigma={\tau^{*}}
for some sort
\tau
, there are two sub-cases.

(a)

If
\tau
is neither in
{\bf Struct}_{\Sigma_{n+1}}
nor in
{\bf{Array}}_{\Sigma}
, then according to
Table

6
,
{\tau^{*}}\notin{\bf Struct}_{\Sigma_{n+1}^{{\phi}}}
. We want to make sure that the cardinality of
{\tau^{*}}^{\cal C}
is infinite, so if
{\tau^{*}}^{\cal B}
is finite, we add infinitely many fresh elements to it, denoted as
\{e_{k}^{{\tau^{*}}}\mid k\in\mathbb{N}\}
.
If
{\tau^{*}}^{\cal B}
is already infinite, we simply set
{\tau^{*}}^{\cal C}={\tau^{*}}^{\cal B}
.

(b)

Otherwise,
\tau
is in
{\bf{Array}}_{\Sigma}
, or in
{\bf Struct}_{\Sigma_{n+1}}
, and thus, according to
Table

6
,
{\tau^{*}}\in{\bf Struct}_{\Sigma_{n+1}^{{\phi}}}
, and we interpret
{\tau^{*}}
in
{\cal C}
as the set of trees defined in
Definition

5
. (Note that the interpretations of all the sorts in
{\bf Elem}_{\Sigma^{{{\phi}}}}
have already been defined in
{\cal C}
as described in items 1 and 2a above, corresponding to the structure specified by
Table

6
).

3.

If
\sigma=A_{i}^{\prime}
for some
i\in[1,n]
, we interpret
A_{i}^{\prime}
in
{\cal C}
as the set of functions from
{I_{i}^{*}}^{\cal C}
to
{E_{i}^{*}}^{\cal C}
.

By
Lemma

B.62
we have
\forall\sigma\in{\cal S}_{\Sigma}.{\sigma^{*}}^{{\cal B}}\subseteq{\sigma^{*}}^{{\cal C}}
.

2. Defining the interpretation of the variables.

For every
\sigma\in{\cal S}_{\Sigma^{{{\phi}}}}
, we choose an arbitrary element
\mathbf{d}_{\sigma}\in\sigma^{{\cal C}}
.

We also create for every
i\in[1,n]
a mapping

\Psi_{i}:A_{i}^{\prime{\cal B}}\rightarrow A_{i}^{\prime{\cal C}}
.
Let there be
x\in A_{i}^{\prime{\cal B}}
.
Note that
A_{i}^{\prime{\cal C}}
is the set of functions from
{I_{i}^{*}}^{{\cal C}}
to
{E_{i}^{*}}^{{\cal C}}
, and thus we can define
\Psi_{i}(x):{I_{i}^{*}}^{{\cal C}}\to{E_{i}^{*}}^{{\cal C}}
in the following way:

\forall j\in{I_{i}^{*}}^{{\cal C}}.\Psi_{i}(x)(j)=\begin{cases}\textbf{select}_{A_{i}^{\prime}}^{\cal B}(x,j)&\text{if }j\in{I_{i}^{*}}^{{\cal B}}\\
\mathbf{d}_{{E_{i}^{*}}}&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

This is well defined since

\textbf{select}_{A_{i}^{\prime}}^{\cal B}(x,j)\in{E_{i}^{*}}^{{\cal B}}\subseteq{E_{i}^{*}}^{{\cal C}}
for every
j\in{I_{i}^{*}}^{{\cal B}}
, and
\mathbf{d}_{{E_{i}^{*}}}\in{E_{i}^{*}}^{{\cal C}}
.

The mapping
\Psi_{i}
is an injection:
let there be
a,b\in A_{i}^{\prime{\cal B}}
such that
a\neq b
.
Then, there exists
j\in{I_{i}^{*}}^{{\cal B}}
such that
\textbf{select}_{A_{i}^{\prime}}^{{\cal B}}(a,j)\neq\textbf{select}_{A_{i}^{\prime}}^{{\cal B}}(b,j)
.
Thus,
\Psi_{i}(a)(j)\neq\Psi_{i}(b)(j)
.

The variables are interpreted in
{\cal C}
as follows:

1.

For every array variable
a
of sort
A_{i}^{\prime}
, we set
a^{{\cal C}}=\Psi_{i}(a^{{\cal B}})
.

2.

For every variable of any other sort (
{\cal S}_{\Sigma^{{\phi}}}\setminus{\bf{Array}}_{\Sigma^{{\phi}}}
) , we set
x^{\cal C}=x^{\cal B}
. This is well defined since we have shown that
\forall\sigma\in{\cal S}_{\Sigma}.{\sigma^{*}}^{{\cal B}}\subseteq{\sigma^{*}}^{{\cal C}}
.

3. Defining the interpretation of the functions and predicates.

The function symbols in
{\cal C}
are interpreted as follows:

1.

For every
i\in[1,n]
, the
\textbf{select}_{A_{i}^{\prime}}
function symbol is interpreted as

\textbf{select}_{A_{i}^{\prime}}^{{\cal C}}(a,j)=a(j)

2.

For every
i\in[1,n]
, the
\textbf{store}_{A_{i}^{\prime}}
function symbol is interpreted as

\textbf{store}_{A_{i}^{\prime}}^{{\cal C}}(a,j,v)(k)=\begin{cases}v&\text{if }k=j\\
a(k)&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

3.

The constructor symbols are interpreted according to the datatype theory.

4.

The selector symbols are interpreted as follows (
s_{(c,i)}:{\sigma^{*}}\to{\tau^{*}}
when
\sigma,\tau\in{\cal S}_{\Sigma}
):

s_{(c,i)}^{{\cal C}}(x)=\begin{cases}t_{i}&\text{if }x=c(t_{1},\dots,t_{n})\\
s_{(c,i)}^{{\cal B}}(x)&\text{otherwise, if }x\in{\sigma^{*}}^{{\cal B}}\\
\mathbf{d}_{{\tau^{*}}}&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

Note that this function is well defined since
{\tau^{*}}^{{\cal B}}\subseteq{\tau^{*}}^{{\cal C}}
and thus
s_{(c,i)}^{{\cal B}}(x)\in{\tau^{*}}^{{\cal C}}
when
x\in{\sigma^{*}}^{{\cal B}}
.

5.

For every function symbol
f_{A_{i}}
of arity
{A_{i}^{*}}\to A_{i}^{\prime}
, we define:

f_{A_{i}}^{{\cal C}}(x)=\begin{cases}\Psi_{i}(f_{A_{i}}^{{\cal B}}(x))&\text{if }x\in{A_{i}^{*}}^{{\cal B}}\\
\mathbf{d}_{A_{i}^{\prime}}&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

Note that this function is well defined since
f_{A_{i}}^{{\cal B}}:{A_{i}^{*}}^{{\cal B}}\to A_{i}^{\prime{\cal B}}
and
\Psi_{i}:A_{i}^{\prime{\cal B}}\to A_{i}^{\prime{\cal C}}
. Thus, we have that
\Psi_{i}(f_{A_{i}}^{{\cal B}}(x))\in A_{i}^{\prime{\cal C}}
for every
x\in{A_{i}^{*}}^{{\cal B}}
.

6.

For every function symbol
g_{A_{i}}
of arity
A_{i}^{\prime}\to{A_{i}^{*}}
, we set

g_{A_{i}}^{{\cal C}}(x)=\begin{cases}g_{A_{i}}^{{\cal B}}(\Psi_{i}^{-1}(x))&\text{if }\Psi_{i}^{-1}(x)\neq\emptyset\\
\mathbf{d}_{{A_{i}^{*}}}&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

.

Note that this function is well defined.
First, we have already shown that
\Psi_{i}
is injective, so
\Psi_{i}^{-1}(x)
contains a single element, if any.
Second, since
\Psi_{i}\colon A_{i}^{\prime{\cal B}}\to A_{i}^{\prime{\cal C}}
, we know that the restricted inverse

\Psi_{i}^{-1}\!\!\upharpoonright_{\{y\in A_{i}^{\prime{\cal C}}\mid\Psi_{i}^{-1}(y)\neq\emptyset\}}\colon\{y\in A_{i}^{\prime{\cal C}}\mid\Psi_{i}^{-1}(y)\neq\emptyset\}\to A_{i}^{\prime{\cal B}}

is well defined.

Thus, as

g_{A_{i}}^{{\cal B}}:A_{i}^{\prime{\cal B}}\to{A_{i}^{*}}^{{\cal B}}
and
{A_{i}^{*}}^{{\cal B}}\subseteq{A_{i}^{*}}^{{\cal C}}
, we have that

g_{A_{i}}^{{\cal B}}(\Psi_{i}^{-1}(x))\in{A_{i}^{*}}^{{\cal C}}

when
\Psi_{i}^{-1}(x)\neq\emptyset
.

7.

The tester symbols are interpreted in the standard way for datatype structors:

\forall c\in{{\cal C}}{{\cal O}}_{\Sigma^{{{\phi}}}}.\;\text{is}_{c}^{{\cal C}}(x)=\begin{cases}true&\text{if }x=c(\ldots)\\
false&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

By the way
{\cal C}
interprets the
\textbf{select}_{A_{j}^{\prime}}
and
\textbf{store}_{A_{j}^{\prime}}
for every
j\in[1,n]
, we get that
{\cal C}^{{\Sigma_{j}^{*}}}
is an array
{\Sigma_{j}^{*}}
-structure.
Likewise,
{\cal C}^{\Sigma^{{{\phi}}}}
is a datatype
\Sigma^{{{\phi}}}
-structure.

3. Showing that
{\cal C}
is a
T_{\text{new}}^{{{\phi}}}
-interpretation.

Recall that

T_{\text{new}}^{{{\phi}}}\;=\;\Bigl(\biguplus_{j=1}^{n}{T_{j}^{*}}\Bigr)\;\uplus\;T_{n+1}^{{\phi}}\;\uplus\;T_{n+2}\,.

For each
j\in[1,n]
, the reduct
{\cal C}^{{\Sigma_{j}^{*}}}
interprets

\textbf{select}_{A_{j}^{\prime}}
and
\textbf{store}_{A_{j}^{\prime}}
in the standard way for an arrays structure.
On
\Sigma_{n+1}^{{\phi}}
,
{\cal C}
uses the usual tree‐based interpretation of
constructors, selectors and testers (see Definition
6
). By
construction, each selector and tester in
\Sigma_{n+1}^{{\phi}}
obeys the requirements of datatypes structures.
Finally, on
\Sigma_{n+2}
we only have the symbols
f_{A_{j}}
and
g_{A_{j}}
,
and there is no restriction on their interpretation.

4. Showing that
{\cal C}
satisfies every literal in
{{\phi}}
.

Let
\ell
be a literal in
{{\phi}}
.
Suppose
{\cal B}
satisfies
\ell
.
We prove
{\cal C}
satisfies it,
by considering all of its possible shapes.

1.

For a literal of the form
x=y
, there are two cases:

(a)

If the sort of
x
and
y
is
A_{i}^{\prime}
, then:

x^{{\cal C}}=\Psi_{i}(x^{{\cal B}})=\Psi_{i}(y^{{\cal B}})=y^{{\cal C}}.

(b)

If the sort of
x
and
y
is not
A_{i}^{\prime}
, then we have that

x^{{\cal C}}=x^{{\cal B}}=y^{{\cal B}}=y^{{\cal C}}.

2.

For a literal of the form
x\neq y
, there are two cases:

(a)

If the sort of
x
and
y
is
A_{i}^{\prime}
, the injectivity of
\Psi_{i}
ensures that:

x^{{\cal C}}=\Psi_{i}(x^{{\cal B}})\neq\Psi_{i}(y^{{\cal B}})=y^{{\cal C}}.

(b)

If the sort of
x
and
y
is not
A_{i}^{\prime}
, then we have that

x^{{\cal C}}=x^{{\cal B}}\neq y^{{\cal B}}=y^{{\cal C}}.

3.

For a literal of the form
x=\textbf{select}_{A_{i}^{\prime}}(a,j)
, we have that

x^{{\cal C}}=x^{{\cal B}}=\textbf{select}_{A_{i}^{\prime}}^{{\cal B}}(a^{{\cal B}},j^{{\cal B}})=a^{{\cal C}}(j^{{\cal C}})=\textbf{select}_{A_{i}^{\prime}}^{{\cal C}}(a^{{\cal C}},j^{{\cal C}}).

The first equality holds since
x\in{\it vars}_{E_{i}}({{{\psi}}})
and thus
x^{{\cal C}}=x^{{\cal B}}
.
The second equality holds because
{\cal B}
satisfies the literal
x=\textbf{select}_{A_{i}^{\prime}}(a,j)
. The third equality holds by the definition of array variables in
{\cal C}
.
The fourth equality holds by the interpretation of the
\textbf{select}_{A_{i}^{\prime}}
function symbol in
{\cal C}
.

4.

For a literal of the form
b=\textbf{store}_{A_{i}^{\prime}}(a,i,v)
, we already know that

b^{{\cal B}}=\textbf{store}_{A_{i}^{\prime}}^{{\cal B}}(a^{{\cal B}},i^{{\cal B}},v^{{\cal B}}).

Thus:

(a)

\textbf{select}_{A_{i}^{\prime}}^{{\cal B}}(b^{{\cal B}},i^{{\cal B}})=v^{{\cal B}}
.

(b)

\forall k\in{I_{i}^{*}}^{{\cal B}}\setminus\{i^{{\cal B}}\}.\textbf{select}_{A_{i}^{\prime}}^{{\cal B}}(b^{{\cal B}},k)=\textbf{select}_{A_{i}^{\prime}}^{{\cal B}}(a^{{\cal B}},k).

Let there be
k\in{I_{i}^{*}}^{{\cal C}}
.
Since,
I_{i}\in{\cal S}_{\Sigma}\setminus({\bf Struct}\cup{\bf{Array}})
, we have that
{I_{i}^{*}}^{{\cal B}}\subseteq{I_{i}^{*}}^{{\cal C}}
. Hence,

{I_{i}^{*}}^{{\cal C}}=\{i^{{\cal C}}\}\cup({I_{i}^{*}}^{{\cal B}}\setminus\{i^{{\cal C}}\})\cup({I_{i}^{*}}^{{\cal C}}\setminus{I_{i}^{*}}^{{\cal B}})
and since
i^{\cal C}=i^{\cal B}
there are three cases:

(a)

If
k=i^{{\cal C}}
, then

\textbf{store}_{A_{i}^{\prime}}^{{\cal C}}(a^{{\cal C}},i^{{\cal C}},v^{{\cal C}})(k)=v^{{\cal C}}=v^{{\cal B}}=\textbf{select}_{A_{i}^{\prime}}^{{\cal B}}(b^{{\cal B}},i^{\cal B})=b^{{\cal C}}(k).

The first equality holds by the definition of
\textbf{store}_{A_{i}^{\prime}}^{{\cal C}}
, the second equality holds since the
v^{{\cal C}}\in{E_{i}^{*}}^{{\cal C}}
, and thus
v^{{\cal C}}=v^{{\cal B}}
, the third equality is the first equality derived from the fact that
b^{{\cal B}}=\textbf{store}_{A_{i}^{\prime}}^{{\cal B}}(a^{{\cal B}},i^{{\cal B}},v^{{\cal B}})
, and the last equality holds by the definition of
\textbf{select}_{A_{i}^{\prime}}^{{\cal C}}
and by substituting
k=i^{{\cal C}}=i^{{\cal B}}
.

(b)

If
k\in{I_{i}^{*}}^{{\cal B}}\setminus\{i^{{\cal C}}\}
, then

\textbf{store}_{A_{i}^{\prime}}^{{\cal C}}(a^{{\cal C}},i^{{\cal C}},v^{{\cal C}})(k)=a^{{\cal C}}(k)=\textbf{select}_{A_{i}^{\prime}}^{{\cal B}}(a^{{\cal B}},k)=\textbf{select}_{A_{i}^{\prime}}^{{\cal B}}(b^{{\cal B}},k)=b^{{\cal C}}(k).

(c)

If
k\in{I_{i}^{*}}^{{\cal C}}\setminus{I_{i}^{*}}^{{\cal B}}
, then

\textbf{store}_{A_{i}^{\prime}}^{{\cal C}}(a^{{\cal C}},i^{{\cal C}},v^{{\cal C}})(k)=a^{{\cal C}}(k)=\mathbf{d}_{{E_{i}^{*}}}=b^{{\cal C}}(k).

Thus, because of the extensionality axiom, we have that

b^{{\cal C}}=\textbf{store}_{A_{i}^{\prime}}^{{\cal C}}(a^{{\cal C}},i^{{\cal C}},v^{{\cal C}})

5.

For a literal of the form
y=c(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})
, we have that

y^{{\cal C}}=y^{{\cal B}}=c^{{\cal B}}(x_{1}^{{\cal B}},\ldots,x_{n}^{{\cal B}})=c^{{\cal C}}(x_{1}^{{\cal C}},\ldots,x_{n}^{{\cal C}})

6.

For a literal of the form
x=s_{(c,i)}(y)
, we have that

x^{{\cal C}}=x^{{\cal B}}=s_{(c,i)}^{{\cal B}}(y^{{\cal B}})=s_{(c,i)}^{{\cal C}}(y^{{\cal C}})

7.

For a literal of the form
\text{is}_{c}(y)
or
\neg\text{is}_{c}(y)
, we have that
y^{{\cal C}}
is an application of a constructor
c
if and only if
y^{{\cal B}}
is an application of a constructor
c
.

8.

For a literal of the form
a=f_{A_{i}}(x)
, we have that

a^{{\cal C}}=\Psi_{i}(a^{{\cal B}})=\Psi_{i}(f_{A}^{{\cal B}}(x^{{\cal B}}))=f_{A}^{{\cal C}}(x^{{\cal C}}).

9.

For a literal of the form
x=g_{A_{i}}(a)
, we have that

x^{{\cal C}}=x^{{\cal B}}=g_{A}^{{\cal B}}(a^{{\cal B}})=g_{A}^{{\cal B}}(\Psi_{i}^{-1}(\Psi_{i}(a^{{\cal B}})))=g_{A}^{{\cal C}}(a^{{\cal C}}).

Domains

{I^{*}}^{{\cal B}_{1}}=\mathbb{N}

{{{A}^{\circ}}}^{{\cal B}_{1}}=\{\gamma\}\;\cup\;\{e_{k}^{{{A}^{\circ}}}\mid k\in\mathbb{N}\}

{E^{*}}^{{\cal B}_{1}}=T_{{E^{*}}}\bigl({\Sigma_{\mathbf{ndt}}^{{{\psi}}}}_{\mid{{\cal C}}{{\cal O}}_{\Sigma_{\mathbf{ndt}}^{{{\psi}}}}},\,B\bigr)

{A^{*}}^{{\cal B}_{1}}=T_{{A^{*}}}\bigl({\Sigma_{\mathbf{ndt}}^{{{\psi}}}}_{\mid{{\cal C}}{{\cal O}}_{\Sigma_{\mathbf{ndt}}^{{{\psi}}}}},\,B\bigr)

Functions

: standard read/update on
A^{\prime{\cal B}_{1}}
.

{{\mathbf{c}}^{*}}^{{\cal B}_{1}}(n,y)={{\mathbf{c}}^{*}}(n,y)

,

,

: constant function returning

\text{is}_{{{\mathbf{c}}^{*}}}^{{\cal B}_{1}}({{\mathbf{c}}^{*}}(n,y))=\text{true}
,

,

\text{is}_{{{\mathbf{emp}}^{*}}}^{{\cal B}_{1}}({{\mathbf{emp}}^{*}})=\text{true}
,

.

Variables

{y^{*}}^{{\cal B}_{1}}={{\mathbf{c}}^{*}}\bigl(1,\;c_{1}(\gamma,{{\mathbf{c}}^{*}}(3,c_{1}(\gamma,{{\mathbf{emp}}^{*}})))\bigr)

Table 21:
A
\Sigma_{\mathbf{ndt}}^{{{\psi}}}
–interpretation
{\cal B}_{1}
.
Here
B
is the
{\bf Elem}_{\Sigma_{\mathbf{ndt}}^{{{\psi}}}}
–sorted set with
B_{\rho}=\rho^{{\cal B}_{1}}
for every
\rho\in{\bf Elem}_{\Sigma_{\mathbf{ndt}}^{{{\psi}}}}
.

Example B.66
.

Consider
\hat{{\cal B}}
from
Table

18
, which satisfies the formula
\bf{trFormula}({{\psi}})
(
Table

12
).
According to
Lemma

B.64
, we can construct a
\Sigma_{\mathbf{ndt}}^{{{\psi}}}
-interpretation
{\cal B}_{1}
that satisfies the same formula, and the three
conditions specified in
Lemma

B.64
.
This new interpretation
{\cal B}_{1}
is defined in
Table

21
.

Sort type

Base case (
i=0
)

Inductive step

\displaystyle\sigma\in{\cal S}_{\Sigma}\setminus({\bf{Array}}_{\Sigma}\cup{\bf Struct}_{\Sigma_{n+1}})

\displaystyle\sigma^{0}={\sigma^{*}}^{{\cal B}}

\displaystyle\sigma^{i+1}={\sigma^{*}}^{{\cal B}}

\displaystyle\sigma=A_{j}\;(j\in[1,n])

\displaystyle\sigma^{0}=\emptyset

\sigma^{i}
for each sort

.

,
